=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) and an integer X.
Print one triple of integers (i, j, k) satisfying all of the following conditions. If no such triple exists, report that fact.

- 1 \leq i \lt j \lt k \leq N
- A_i + A_j + A_k = X

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N X
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

If there exists an integer triple (i, j, k) satisfying the conditions, print one in the following format. If there are multiple solutions, you may print any of them.
i j k

If no such triple exists, print -1.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 10^6
- 1 \leq X \leq 10^6
- 1 \leq A_i \leq X
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

5 16
1 8 5 10 13

Sample Output 1

1 3 4

The triple (i, j, k) = (1, 3, 4) satisfies 1 \leq i \lt j \lt k \leq N and A_i + A_j + A_k = 1 + 5 + 10 = 16 = X.

Sample Input 2

5 20
1 8 5 10 13

Sample Output 2

-1

Sample Input 3

10 100000
73766 47718 74148 49218 76721 31902 21994 18880 29598 98917

Sample Output 3

4 6 8

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

I recall a technique for 3SUM with bounded values: Use "bitset" to compute pair sums for small values, and enumerate large values. Specifically, choose threshold B = sqrt(X). Let "small" values <= B. For triples with at least two small values, enumerate pairs of small values. For triples with at most one small value, then at least two values > B. Since values > B, their sum > 2B. The remaining value = X - sum. Maybe we can enumerate the large values? Let's derive.

Classic algorithm:
for a in range(D-2):
  l=a+1; r=D-1
  while l<r:
    s=vals[a]+vals[l]+vals[r]
    if s==X found
    elif s<X: l+=1
    else: r-=1

Let's design NTT.

We need multiply two polynomials:
- Poly A: length L, A[v] = 1 if value v present (count>=1) for v=1..X, else 0. A[0]=0.
- Poly B same. We need convolution C = A * A. Since same, we can square using one NTT: NTT(A), pointwise square, inverse. But NTT of A once, square, inverse. That's 2 forward? Actually NTT(A) (forward), square, inverse. Need one forward and one inverse. Good.

NTT implementation iterative Cooley-Tukey.

Mod = 998244353, primitive root g=3.

We can precompute roots? For each stage, we need roots. Standard iterative NTT:

def ntt(a, invert):
    n = len(a)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1,n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(root, (mod-1)//length, mod)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, mod-2, mod)
        for i in range(0,n,length):
            w = 1
            half = length >> 1
            for j in range(i, i+half):
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j+half] * w % mod
                a[j] = (u+v) % mod
                a[j+half] = (u-v) % mod
                w = w * wlen % mod
        length <<= 1
    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, mod-2, mod)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % mod

This bit-reversal O(n log n)? Actually bit-reversal O(n). The nested loops total n/2 * log n. For n=2M, okay. But Python while bit-reversal for 2M maybe okay. However using `% mod` inside inner loop for every butterfly can be slow. We can optimize.

Alternative use AtCoder Library style NTT with precomputed roots and no bit reversal? There is a well-known fast NTT implementation in Python:

```
sum_e = [...]
sum_ie = [...]
def butterfly(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = (n-1).bit_length()
    length = 0
    while length < h:
        if h - length == 1:
            p = 1 << (h - length - 1)
            rot = 1
            for s in range(1 << length):
                offset = s << (h - length)
                for i in range(p):
                    l = a[i+offset]
                    r = a[i+offset+p] * rot % mod
                    a[i+offset] = (l+r)%mod
                    a[i+offset+p] = (l-r)%mod
                rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length()-1] % mod
            length += 1
        else:
            ...
```
This is more complex but faster.

Let's consider using Python's `int` bitset with "meet-in-the-middle" and block decomposition for pair sums. Maybe we can compute pair sum existence for all S in O(X * sqrt(X) / word) using blocks, which might be faster than NTT and simpler.

Block decomposition for boolean convolution:
Let B = 1024. Split values into blocks of size B. Number blocks M = ceil(X/B) ≈ 977.
For each block p, define bitset `block_bits[p]` of values in that block (B bits). We want pair sum bitset P where bit S = 1 if ∃b,c present with b+c=S.
We can compute P by considering pairs of blocks (p,q). For each block p, q, the sums from b in block p and c in block q form a range. We need OR over b in p, c in q of 1 << (b+c). This is like convolution of two small sets (size B). We could precompute for each pair of blocks? M^2 ~1e6 pairs, each convolution of B-length sets. Too much.

But we can compute P by iterating over blocks p and using precomputed `shifted_or` for each block? For each block p, we want OR_{b in block p} (B_bits << b). This is a bitset of length 2X. Precomputing for each block costs B shifts per block -> D shifts total, too slow. But maybe we can precompute within a block using bitset of block? Since B=1024, for each block, we can compute all pair sums within block and with other blocks? Hmm.

Alternatively, use "bitset convolution by splitting into high/low bits":

Actually AtCoder convolution Python code:

```
def primitive_root(m):
    if m == 2: return 1
    divs = []
    x = m-1
    i=2
    while i*i<=x:
        if x%i==0:
            divs.append(i)
            while x%i==0: x//=i
        i+=1
    if x>1: divs.append(x)
    g=2
    while True:
        ok=True
        for d in divs:
            if pow(g,(m-1)//d,m)==1:
                ok=False; break
        if ok: return g
        g+=1
```

But for mod 998244353, g=3.

```
def butterfly(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = (n - 1).bit_length()
    le = 0
    while le < h:
        if h - le == 1:
            p = 1 << (h - le - 1)
            rot = 1
            for s in range(1 << le):
                offset = s << (h - le)
                for i in range(p):
                    l = a[i + offset]
                    r = a[i + offset + p] * rot % MOD
                    a[i + offset] = (l + r) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + p] = (l - r) % MOD
                if s + 1 != (1 << le):
                    rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length() - 1] % MOD
            le += 1
        else:
            p = 1 << (h - le - 2)
            rot = 1
            imag = primitive_root?

Implement fast NTT. Let's write a robust iterative NTT with bit-reversal. Maybe optimize by precomputing roots per stage? We can compute `wlen = pow(root, (MOD-1)//length, MOD)` for each stage; only 21 pow calls. Inner loop uses `w = w * wlen % MOD` for each butterfly. That's one multiplication for w update plus one for v. 22M w updates + 22M v multiplications =44M multiplications. Could optimize by precomputing roots for each stage in a list and using them? Alternative use "decimation in frequency" NTT without bit reversal? There is an iterative NTT that avoids bit-reversal at start if using ACL butterfly. Let's implement ACL-style to be faster.

Let's recall AtCoder Library Python convolution code (from atcoder library):

```
MOD = 998244353
G = 3

sum_e = [0] * 30
sum_ie = [0] * 30
es = [0] * 30
ies = [0] * 30
cnt2 = (MOD - 1) & -(MOD - 1)
e = pow(G, (MOD - 1) // cnt2, MOD)
ie = pow(e, MOD - 2, MOD)
for i in range(30):
    es[i] = e
    ies[i] = ie
    e = e * e % MOD
    ie = ie * ie % MOD
now = 1
inow = 1
for i in range(30):
    sum_e[i] = es[i] * now % MOD
    sum_ie[i] = ies[i] * inow % MOD
    now = now * ies[i] % MOD
    inow = inow * es[i] % MOD
```

Then `butterfly(a)`:

```
def butterfly(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = (n - 1).bit_length()
    length = 0
    while length < h:
        if h - length == 1:
            p = 1 << (h - length - 1)
            rot = 1
            for s in range(1 << length):
                offset = s << (h - length)
                for i in range(p):
                    l = a[i + offset]
                    r = a[i + offset + p] * rot % MOD
                    a[i + offset] = (l + r) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + p] = (l - r) % MOD
                if s + 1 != (1 << length):
                    rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length() - 1] % MOD
            length += 1
        else:
            p = 1 << (h - length - 2)
            rot = 1
            imag = sum_e[1]  # primitive 4th root?
            for s in range(1 << length):
                rot2 = rot * rot % MOD
                rot3 = rot2 * rot % MOD
                offset = s << (h - length)
                for i in range(p):
                    a0 = a[i + offset]
                    a1 = a[i + offset + p] * rot
                    a2 = a[i + offset + 2*p] * rot2
                    a3 = a[i + offset + 3*p] * rot3
                    a1na3imag = (a1 - a3) % MOD * imag
                    a[i + offset] = (a0 + a2 + a1 + a3) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + p] = (a0 + a2 - a1 - a3) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + 2*p] = (a0 - a2 + a1na3imag) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + 3*p] = (a0 - a2 - a1na3imag) % MOD
                if s + 1 != (1 << length):
                    rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length() - 1] % MOD
            length += 2
```

And `butterfly_inv(a)` similar with sum_ie and inv imag. This is complex but faster (radix-4). Maybe too error-prone.

Simpler bit-reversal NTT is safer. We can still optimize:
- Precompute bit-reversal permutation? For n=2M, list of ints length 2M memory 16MB refs + int objects? Could use array('i') 8MB. But computing bit-reversal on the fly in NTT is O(n) and avoids memory. The while loop for bit reversal is okay.
- Use local variables for MOD, a, etc.
- Use `% MOD` after addition/subtraction. We can reduce modulo operations:
  - `x = u + v; if x >= MOD: x -= MOD`
  - `y = u - v; if y < 0: y += MOD`
  Since u,v in [0,MOD), u+v < 2MOD, u-v > -MOD. Good.
  - For multiplication, `v = a[j+half] * w % MOD`.
  - w update `w = w * wlen % MOD`.

Now, NTT function with list `a` of length L. Let's implement carefully.

```
MOD = 998244353
G = 3

def ntt(a, invert):
    n = len(a)
    # bit-reversal
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(G, (MOD - 1) // length, MOD)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, MOD - 2, MOD)
        half = length >> 1
        for i in range(0, n, length):
            w = 1
            end = i + half
            for j in range(i, end):
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j + half] * w % MOD
                x = u + v
                if x >= MOD:
                    x -= MOD
                y = u - v
                if y < 0:
                    y += MOD
                a[j] = x
                a[j + half] = y
                w = w * wlen % MOD
        length <<= 1
    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, MOD - 2, MOD)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % MOD
```
For inverse, using same bit-reversal and wlen inverse works. Good.

Could optimize by computing `wlen` for inverse as `pow(G, MOD-1 - (MOD-1)//length, MOD)`? `pow(wlen, MOD-2, MOD)` okay 21 times.

Potential issue: In inverse NTT, the standard algorithm with bit-reversal at start and inverse roots works. Yes.

...
a[j+half] = y
```

Now, NTT performance with bit-reversal. We can optimize by using local variables:
```
def ntt(a, invert):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(G, (mod - 1) // length, mod)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, mod - 2, mod)
        half = length >> 1
        for i in range(0, n, length):
            w = 1
            j = i
            end = i + half
            while j < end:
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j + half] * w % mod
                x = u + v
                if x >= mod:
                    x -= mod
                y = u - v
                if y < 0:
                    y += mod
                a[j] = x
                a[j + half] = y
                w = w * wlen % mod
                j += 1
        length <<= 1
    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, mod - 2, mod)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % mod
```
Using `while` instead of `for` maybe faster? In Python, `for range` is faster than while. Use `for j in range(i, end):`. But need `j+half`; okay.

```
for j in range(i, i+half):
    u = a[j]
    v = a[j+half] * w % mod
    ...
    w = w * wlen % mod
```

Let's recalc max if all off-diagonal pairs involve av and diagonal present: off-diagonal involving av contributes 2, diagonal contributes 1, total 3. Ah! Earlier I said coef<=2, but if S=2*av? Wait off-diagonal involving av requires S-av != av. If S=2av, pair (av,av) is diagonal, not off-diagonal. If S != 2av, off-diagonal involving av contributes 2. Diagonal can also contribute 1 if S even and S/2 present. Could S/2 equal av? That would S=2av, but then off-diagonal involving av impossible. If S != 2av, diagonal value S/2 is not av. Could there be diagonal plus off-diagonal involving av? Example S=6, av=2. Off-diagonal involving av: (2,4),(4,2) sum6. Diagonal: (3,3) sum6. Total coef=3. All off-diagonal pairs involve av (the pair (2,4)), diagonal (3,3) not involving av. Does this imply a distinct triple? For av=2, S=6, X=av+S=8. Pair (3,3) diagonal would give triple 2+3+3=8, which is repeated (two 3s). Repeated detection would have handled if cnt[3]>=2. If cnt[3]=1, invalid. Pair (2,4) involves av, invalid for distinct. So coef=3 does NOT guarantee a pair not involving av! My earlier threshold should be coef >=4? Let's analyze.

We need pair of distinct values not involving av. Ordered pairs:
- Off-diagonal not involving av: contribute 2 per unordered pair.
- Off-diagonal involving av: contribute 2 per unordered pair (av, other).
- Diagonal: contribute 1 if S even and S/2 present.

If all off-diagonal pairs involve av, there is at most 1 such unordered pair (av, other) (if other != av), contributing 2. Diagonal can add 1. Total max 3. Thus coef >=4 guarantees a pair not involving av. If coef == 3, could be off-diagonal involving av + diagonal. If diagonal value has count>=2, repeated handled; if count=1, invalid. Could also be one off-diagonal not involving av (2) + diagonal (1) =3, valid. Need distinguish.

So our condition should be:
- coef >= 4: definitely valid.
- coef == 3: need check. Could be valid (one off-diagonal not involving av + diagonal) or invalid (off-diagonal involving av + diagonal). We can determine by checking if `other = S-av` is present and `other != av`. If yes, then off-diagonal involving av exists. Since coef==3, the remaining 1 is diagonal, no other off-diagonal. - coef == 2: as before, if other present and other != av, invalid; else valid.
- coef == 1: diagonal only, invalid.

What about coef == 3 with other present but other == av? That would S=2av, off-diagonal involving av impossible; other==av means diagonal? Actually if other==av, then S-av=av -> S=2av. The pair (av,av) is diagonal, not off-diagonal. coef==3 would be diagonal (1) + one off-diagonal not involving av (2), valid. Our check `other != av` handles.

    continue
if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
    other = S - av
    if other != av and 1 <= other <= X and cnt[other] > 0:
        continue
# coef >=4 or valid 2/3
```

Now, bit-reversal while loop:
```
j = 0
for i in range(1, n):
    bit = n >> 1
    while j & bit:
        j ^= bit
        bit >>= 1
    j ^= bit
    if i < j:
        a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
```
This is standard. For n=2M, the while loop total ~n. Good.

Now, final code structure:

```
import sys
from array import array

MOD = 998244353
G = 3
SMALL = 5000

def ints():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    num = 0
    in_num = False
    for c in data:
        if 48 <= c <= 57:
            num = num * 10 + c - 48
            in_num = True
        else:
            if in_num:
                yield num
                num = 0
                in_num = False
    if in_num:
        yield num

def ntt(a, invert):
    ...

def main():
    it = ints()
    try:
        N = next(it)
    except StopIteration:
        return
    X = next(it)
    cnt = [0]*(X+1)
    pos1 = array('i', [0])*(X+1)
    pos2 = array('i', [0])*(X+1)
    pos3 = array('i', [0])*(X+1)
    distinct = 0
    for idx in range(1, N+1):
        v = next(it)
        c = cnt[v]
        if c == 0:
            distinct += 1
            pos1[v] = idx
        elif c == 1:
            pos2[v] = idx
        elif c == 2:
            pos3[v] = idx
        cnt[v] = c + 1
    # maybe del it to free input buffer
    del it

    # repeated triples
    for d in range(1, X+1):
        if cnt[d] >= 2:
            s = X - 2*d
            if 1 <= s <= X and cnt[s] >= 1:
                if s == d:
                    if cnt[d] >= 3:
                        ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos3[d]]
                        ans.sort()
                        print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                        return
                else:
                    ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos1[s]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return

    # fallback small distinct
    if distinct <= SMALL:
        vals = [v for v in range(1, X+1) if cnt[v]]
        D = len(vals)
        for i in range(D):
            b = vals[i]
            for j in range(i+1, D):
                c = vals[j]
                a = X - b - c
                if 1 <= a <= X and cnt[a] > 0 and a != b and a != c:
                    ans = [pos1[a], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return
        print(-1)
        return

    # NTT
    L = 1
    while L < 2*X + 1:
        L <<= 1
    a = [0]*L
    for v in range(1, X+1):
        if cnt[v]:
            a[v] = 1
    ntt(a, False)
    for i in range(L):
        a[i] = a[i] * a[i] % MOD
    ntt(a, True)

    for av in range(1, X+1):
        if cnt[av] == 0:
            continue
        S = X - av
        if S < 2:
            continue
        coef = a[S]
        if coef == 0 or coef == 1:
            continue
        if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
            other = S - av
            if other != av and 1 <= other <= X and cnt[other] > 0:
                continue
        # scan
        lo = S - X
        if lo < 1:
            lo = 1
        hi = S // 2
        for b in range(lo, hi + 1):
            if cnt[b] == 0:
                continue
            c = S - b
            if c < 1 or c > X or cnt[c] == 0:
                continue
            if b == c or b == av or c == av:
                continue
            ans = [pos1[av], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
            ans.sort()
            print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
            return
    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Standard iterative NTT:
```
def ntt(a, invert):
    n=len(a)
    j=0
    for i in range(1,n):
       bit=n>>1
       while j & bit:
          j ^= bit
          bit >>=1
       j ^= bit
       if i<j: swap
    len_=2
    while len_ <= n:
       wlen = pow(root, (mod-1)//len_, mod)
       if invert: wlen = pow(wlen, mod-2, mod)
       for i in range(0,n,len_):
          w=1
          for j in range(i, i+len_//2):
             u=a[j]
             v=a[j+len_//2]*w % mod
             a[j]=u+v
             a[j+len_//2]=u-v
             w=w*wlen%mod
       len_ <<=1
    if invert: multiply by inv_n
```
This is correct. Need ensure `u+v` and `u-v` modulo. We do.

Now, let's think about if `wlen` for inverse should be `pow(root, mod-1 - (mod-1)//len_, mod)`? `pow(wlen, mod-2, mod)` is correct.

Now, let's consider if `root=3` is primitive root for 998244353. Yes.

Now, let's think about if `MOD` prime and length divides MOD-1. L=2^21 divides 2^23. Good.

Now, let's consider if in NTT, after forward, pointwise square, inverse, the result is convolution but scaled by n? Inverse includes inv_n. Good.

Now, let's think about if we should use `int` for `a` values; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a risk of recursion? no.

### Issue: Performance of NTT with bit-reversal

Let's consider optimizing NTT further. We can precompute bit-reversal permutation? no.

Maybe use `for i in range(1, n):` bit-reversal while loop. For n=2M, this loop is okay. But the while inside can be many operations. Total bit-reversal swaps ~n/2. Good.

Could use a faster bit-reversal by iterative:
```
j = 0
for i in range(1, n):
    bit = n >> 1
    while j & bit:
        j ^= bit
        bit >>= 1
    j ^= bit
    if i < j: swap
```
This is standard.

Now, inner NTT loops:
```
length = 2
while length <= n:
    wlen = pow(...)
    half = length >> 1
    for i in range(0, n, length):
        w = 1
        for j in range(i, i+half):
            ...
    length <<= 1
```
For n=2M, total inner iterations n/2 * log2 n = 22M. Good.

22M modulo multiplications for v and 22M for w update =44M. Could reduce w update by using precomputed roots for each stage? Not.

Maybe use "root powers" precomputed for each stage in a flat list and avoid w update multiplication? Actually w update is one multiplication per butterfly; precomputing roots would require storing half roots for each stage total n-1 ~2M ints, memory 56MB, and then inner loop uses `w = roots[offset + j]` no multiplication. But memory increases. Time maybe faster. Not necessary.

Could use "if invert" branch outside stage loops? We can have separate forward and inverse functions to avoid checking invert inside? We only call once forward, once inverse. The `if invert` inside stage loop is evaluated 21 times, negligible.

Could use local `a_jh = a[j + half]` etc. Good.

Now, let's think about if `MOD` operations with `%` for multiplication are slow. We can use `v = a[j+half] * w % mod`; necessary.

Now, let's consider if using PyPy, big int multiplication of 30-bit limbs is okay.

Maybe big-int multiplication with base 2^20 and `int.from_bytes` could be simpler and faster? Let's evaluate.

Construct bytearray of length `(K*X + 7)//8` where K=20. For each present v, set bit at position K*v. Since K*v is multiple of 20, not byte-aligned. Need set bit in bytearray:
```
bit_index = K * v
bytearray[bit_index >> 3] |= 1 << (bit_index & 7)
```
D up to 1e6, okay. Then `poly = int.from_bytes(bytearray, 'little')`? Need bit order. If bytearray little-endian, bit position b corresponds to bit b of integer. Yes. Then `prod = poly * poly`. But multiplication of integers in base 2? Wait `poly` as integer has bit positions K*v. Multiplying two such integers as binary integers does NOT compute convolution in base 2^K because carries occur when coefficients overlap? Let's examine. `poly = sum 2^{K*v}`. Product = sum_{b,c} 2^{K(b+c)}. If multiple pairs have same exponent e, the coefficient is number of pairs. In binary integer, if coefficient >=2, it causes carries into higher bits. We need avoid carries by choosing K large enough so coefficient < 2^K. Then within the 20-bit field at exponent e, coefficient fits and no carry to next field. However, binary addition of coefficients within the same field: if coefficient is e.g., 3, it's binary 11 in that field, occupying bits 0 and1 of the field, no carry beyond field because coefficient <2^K. So product integer's bits are exactly the base-2^K digits (coefficients) concatenated. Thus we can extract coefficient at S by `(prod >> (K*S)) & ((1<<K)-1)`. This works! No carries between fields because each coefficient <2^K. Need K such that max coefficient <2^K. Max coefficient for presence set = X =1e6, so K=20 (2^20=1,048,576) enough. But wait, coefficient at S can be X exactly? For S=X+1, number of positive pairs b+c=X+1 is X (1..X). Yes 1,000,000 <1,048,576. Good.

This is brilliant. Could be much faster than NTT? Let's estimate.

Now, let's consider if NTT with bit-reversal and modulo operations might be too slow for a 2s time limit. Maybe we can optimize by using the ACL radix-4 NTT. Let's see if we can implement it correctly. It would reduce operations (radix-4) and maybe faster. But risk of bugs. We can try to write a simpler optimized NTT using precomputed roots and no bit-reversal? Let's recall a known fast NTT implementation in Python:

```
def butterfly(a):
    n = len(a)
    h = (n - 1).bit_length()
    le = 0
    while le < h:
        if h - le == 1:
            p = 1 << (h - le - 1)
            rot = 1
            for s in range(1 << le):
                offset = s << (h - le)
                for i in range(p):
                    l = a[i + offset]
                    r = a[i + offset + p] * rot % MOD
                    a[i + offset] = (l + r) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + p] = (l - r) % MOD
                if s + 1 != (1 << le):
                    rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length() - 1] % MOD
            le += 1
        else:
            p = 1 << (h - le - 2)
            rot = 1
            imag = sum_e[1]
            for s in range(1 << le):
                rot2 = rot * rot % MOD
                rot3 = rot2 * rot % MOD
                offset = s << (h - le)
                for i in range(p):
                    a0 = a[i + offset]
                    a1 = a[i + offset + p] * rot % MOD
                    a2 = a[i + offset + 2*p] * rot2 % MOD
                    a3 = a[i + offset + 3*p] * rot3 % MOD
                    a1na3imag = (a1 - a3) % MOD * imag % MOD
                    a[i + offset] = (a0 + a2 + a1 + a3) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + p] = (a0 + a2 - a1 - a3) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + 2*p] = (a0 - a2 + a1na3imag) % MOD
                    a[i + offset + 3*p] = (a0 - a2 - a1na3imag) % MOD
                if s + 1 != (1 << le):
                    rot = rot * sum_e[(~s & -~s).bit_length() - 1] % MOD
            le += 2
```
This is from atcoder library. It avoids bit-reversal and uses radix-4. But implementing `sum_e` correctly is tricky. The simple bit-reversal NTT is safer.

Maybe we can optimize simple NTT by using `for j in range(i, i + half):` and local variables. Good.

If we truncate polynomials to degree X and use L > X, high-degree terms (degree > L-1) wrap. But if we only care coefficients < L, high-degree terms with degree >= L wrap to degree degree-L, which could be < L and corrupt. So need L > 2X. Unless we use "truncated convolution" with middle product? no.

We can write a fast scanner class that reads all bytes and parses ints with index in a while loop, avoiding generator yield overhead? A class with `next_int` method still Python loop per byte but no yield overhead. Could be faster than generator. Let's implement a simple fast scanner:

```
data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
idx = 0
n_data = len(data)
def next_int():
    nonlocal idx  # not at top
```
At module level, can't nonlocal. We can define inside main:
```
data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
idx = 0
Ldata = len(data)
def ni():
    nonlocal idx
    while data[idx] <= 32:
        idx += 1
    num = 0
    while idx < Ldata and data[idx] > 32:
        num = num*10 + data[idx] - 48
        idx += 1
    return num
```
But `nonlocal` inside main works. This avoids generator yield and bytes split. It still loops over bytes but only digits and spaces; faster than generator? Probably. Need handle idx < Ldata in whitespace loop. Since input valid, can assume. But to avoid IndexError at end, check.

Let's implement inside `main`:
```
data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
idx = 0
L = len(data)
def next_int():
    nonlocal idx
    while idx < L and data[idx] <= 32:
        idx += 1
    num = 0
    while idx < L and data[idx] > 32:
        num = num * 10 + (data[idx] - 48)
        idx += 1
    return num
```
Then `N = next_int(); X = next_int(); ...`

```
def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    idx = 0
    ndata = len(data)
    def next_int():
        nonlocal idx
        while idx < ndata and data[idx] <= 32:
            idx += 1
        num = 0
        while idx < ndata and data[idx] > 32:
            num = num * 10 + data[idx] - 48
            idx += 1
        return num
    N = next_int()
    X = next_int()
    ...
    for idx_pos in range(1, N+1):
        v = next_int()
        ...
    data = None
```
But note variable name `idx` used for parser and loop index; use `p` for parser index and `pos` for position.

```

Let's write NTT carefully:

```
def ntt(a, invert):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(G, (mod - 1) // length, mod)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, mod - 2, mod)
        half = length >> 1
        for i in range(0, n, length):
            w = 1
            end = i + half
            for j in range(i, end):
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j + half] * w % mod
                x = u + v
                if x >= mod:
                    x -= mod
                y = u - v
                if y < 0:
                    y += mod
                a[j] = x
                a[j + half] = y
                w = w * wlen % mod
        length <<= 1
    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, mod - 2, mod)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % mod
```
In inner loop, `a[j + half]` accessed twice? We access once for v, then assign. Good.

Could optimize by storing `ajh = j + half`? no.

Now, let's think about if `w` update after last butterfly in each block is unnecessary; we still do it. Could avoid by looping `for j in range(i, end-1)`? no, need w for each. The last w update wasted. Not significant.

Could precompute `wlen` powers for each stage in a list and use `w = roots[stage][j-i]` to avoid w update multiplication. But memory. Not.

Let's verify with n=4 manually maybe:
Forward NTT of [a0,a1,a2,a3]:
bitrev order [a0,a2,a1,a3]? Actually standard bit-reversal for n=4: i=1 j=2 swap a1,a2 -> [a0,a2,a1,a3].
Stage len=2: blocks (0,1): [a0+a2, a0-a2]; block (2,3): [a1+a3, a1-a3].
Stage len=4: wlen = root^(mod-1)/4 = i. For j=0: u=A0, v=A2*1 -> A0+ A2, A0-A2. j=1: u=A1, v=A3*i -> A1+iA3, A1-iA3. Result [A0+A2, A0-A2, A1+iA3, A1-iA3]? Wait standard NTT output order maybe [F0, F2, F1, F3]? Actually bit-reversal at start and iterative stages yields output in natural order? Let's check. For n=4, input after bitrev [a0,a2,a1,a3]. After stage len=2: [a0+a2, a0-a2, a1+a3, a1-a3]. After stage len=4:
j=0: u=a0+a2, v=a1+a3 -> F0 = a0+a1+a2+a3.
j=1: u=a0-a2, v=(a1-a3)*wlen (wlen=i) -> F1? Standard F1 = a0 + a1*i - a2 - a3*i = (a0-a2) + i(a1-a3). Yes matches.
j=2: u=a0+a2, v=(a1+a3)*wlen^2? Wait for j=2, w = wlen^2 = -1. u=a0+a2, v=-(a1+a3) -> F2 = a0 - a1 + a2 - a3. Good.
j=3: u=a0-a2, v=(a1-a3)*wlen^3 = -i(a1-a3) -> F3 = a0 - i a1 - a2 + i a3. Good. Output natural order. Good.

Inverse with bitrev at start and inverse roots recovers. Good.

### Updated code plan:

```
SMALL = 5000

def main():
    read input...
    small_limit = X // 2

    # repeated detection
    for d in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[d] >= 2:
            s = X - 2*d
            if 1 <= s <= X and cnt[s] >= 1:
                if s == d:
                    if cnt[d] >= 3: output
                else:
                    output

    # fallback over small pairs
    small_vals = [v for v in range(1, small_limit + 1) if cnt[v]]
    M = len(small_vals)
    if M <= SMALL:
        for i in range(M):
            b = small_vals[i]
            for j in range(i+1, M):
                c = small_vals[j]
                a = X - b - c
                if 1 <= a <= X and cnt[a] > 0 and a != b and a != c:
                    output
        print(-1); return

    # NTT on small values
    L = 1
    while L <= X:
        L <<= 1
    a = [0] * L
    for v in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[v]:
            a[v] = 1
    ntt(a, False)
    for i in range(L):
        a[i] = a[i] * a[i] % MOD
    ntt(a, True)

    max_pair = 2 * small_limit
    for av in range(1, X+1):
        if cnt[av] == 0: continue
        S = X - av
        if S < 2 or S > max_pair: continue
        coef = a[S]
        if coef == 0 or coef == 1: continue
        if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
            other = S - av
            if other != av and 1 <= other <= small_limit and cnt[other] > 0:
                continue
        lo = S - small_limit
        if lo < 1: lo = 1
        hi = S // 2
        if lo > hi: continue
        for b in range(lo, hi + 1):
            if cnt[b] == 0: continue
            c = S - b
            if c < 1 or c > small_limit or cnt[c] == 0: continue
            if b == c or b == av or c == av: continue
            ans = [pos1[av], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
            ans.sort()
            print(...)
            return
    print(-1)
```

### Issue: If `data` is set to None after reading, but `next_int` closure still exists and `data` cell is None. If we then define another variable `data`? no.

Now, let's write the final code.

Double-check variable names:
- `p` for parser index.
- `pos` for position.
- `small_limit`.

Potential issue in parser: `data[p] <= 32` when p == len(data) in whitespace loop if next_int called extra; but we don't. In digit loop, condition `p < ndata and data[p] > 32`. Good.

Let's implement `next_int` with `nonlocal p`:
```
def next_int():
    nonlocal p
    while p < ndata and data[p] <= 32:
        p += 1
    num = 0
    while p < ndata and data[p] > 32:
        num = num * 10 + (data[p] - 48)
        p += 1
    return num
```
If input empty, `if not data: return`.

After reading, `data = None` to free. But because `next_int` closure references `data`, assigning `data = None` inside main after function definition is okay? In Python, `data` is a local variable; `next_int` closes over it. Assigning `data = None` in the same scope after the nested function is allowed and changes the cell. However, because `data` is assigned after the nested function, it is still a local variable. Good.

But note: We assign `data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()` before defining `next_int`. Then inside `next_int`, `data` is a free variable. After reading, `data = None` will make `next_int` see None if called, but we don't. Good.

Now, in reading loop, use `pos` variable:
```
for pos in range(1, N + 1):
    v = next_int()
    c = cnt[v]
    if c == 0:
        if v <= small_limit:
            small_distinct += 1
        pos1[v] = pos
    elif c == 1:
        pos2[v] = pos
    elif c == 2:
        pos3[v] = pos
    cnt[v] = c + 1
```
If c==0 and v<=small_limit, small_distinct increments. If c>0, no.

Now, repeated detection:
```
for d in range(1, small_limit + 1):
    if cnt[d] >= 2:
        s = X - 2 * d
        if 1 <= s <= X and cnt[s] >= 1:
            if s == d:
                if cnt[d] >= 3:
                    ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos3[d]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return
            else:
                ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos1[s]]
                ans.sort()
                print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                return
```
If s==d and cnt[d]>=3, output. If s==d and cnt[d]>=2 but <3, no output. If s!=d, output. Good.

Fallback:
```
if small_distinct <= SMALL:
    small_vals = [v for v in range(1, small_limit + 1) if cnt[v]]
    M = len(small_vals)
    for i in range(M):
        b = small_vals[i]
        for j in range(i + 1, M):
            c = small_vals[j]
            a = X - b - c
            if 1 <= a <= X and cnt[a] > 0 and a != b and a != c:
                ans = [pos1[a], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
                ans.sort()
                print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                return
    print(-1)
    return
```
If small_limit=0, small_vals empty, print -1.

NTT:
```
L = 1
while L <= X:
    L <<= 1
a = [0] * L
for v in range(1, small_limit + 1):
    if cnt[v]:
        a[v] = 1
ntt(a, False)
for i in range(L):
    a[i] = a[i] * a[i] % MOD
ntt(a, True)
max_pair = 2 * small_limit
for av in range(1, X + 1):
    if cnt[av] == 0:
        continue
    S = X - av
    if S < 2 or S > max_pair:
        continue
    coef = a[S]
    if coef == 0 or coef == 1:
        continue
    if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
        other = S - av
        if other != av and 1 <= other <= small_limit and cnt[other] > 0:
            continue
    lo = S - small_limit
    if lo < 1:
        lo = 1
    hi = S // 2
    if lo > hi:
        continue
    for b in range(lo, hi + 1):
        if cnt[b] == 0:
            continue
        c = S - b
        if c < 1 or c > small_limit or cnt[c] == 0:
            continue
        if b == c or b == av or c == av:
            continue
        ans = [pos1[av], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
        ans.sort()
        print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
        return
print(-1)
```

Need ensure `a[S]` index S < L. S <= X-1 < L because L > X. Good.

But before final, let's consider if there is any issue with `data = None` and `next_int` closure in PyPy. PyPy may not free memory immediately but okay. Could `del data` be better? If we `del data`, because `data` is a cell variable, the cell becomes empty and the bytes object is decref. But `next_int` closure still references the cell (empty). That's fine. However, if we `del data`, and then later try to access `data` in main? We don't. But `data` is a local variable; `del data` will make the cell empty. The `next_int` function if called would raise NameError? Actually free variable cell empty -> NameError. We don't call. This frees bytes. Assigning `data = None` also frees bytes and keeps cell with None. Both fine. Assigning None is safer if we accidentally reference data? no. Use `data = None`.

Final code:

```python
import sys
from array import array

MOD = 998244353
G = 3
SMALL = 5000

def ntt(a, invert):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]
    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(G, (mod - 1) // length, mod)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, mod - 2, mod)
        half = length >> 1
        for i in range(0, n, length):
            w = 1
            end = i + half
            for j in range(i, end):
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j + half] * w % mod
                x = u + v
                if x >= mod:
                    x -= mod
                y = u - v
                if y < 0:
                    y += mod
                a[j] = x
                a[j + half] = y
                w = w * wlen % mod
        length <<= 1
    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, mod - 2, mod)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % mod

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    if not data:
        return
    p = 0
    ndata = len(data)
    def next_int():
        nonlocal p
        while p < ndata and data[p] <= 32:
            p += 1
        num = 0
        while p < ndata and data[p] > 32:
            num = num * 10 + (data[p] - 48)
            p += 1
        return num

    N = next_int()
    X = next_int()
    small_limit = X // 2

    cnt = [0] * (X + 1)
    pos1 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)
    pos2 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)
    pos3 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)

    small_distinct = 0

    for pos in range(1, N + 1):
        v = next_int()
        c = cnt[v]
        if c == 0:
            if v <= small_limit:
                small_distinct += 1
            pos1[v] = pos
        elif c == 1:
            pos2[v] = pos
        elif c == 2:
            pos3[v] = pos
        cnt[v] = c + 1

    data = None

    # repeated-value triples
    for d in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[d] >= 2:
            s = X - 2 * d
            if 1 <= s <= X and cnt[s] >= 1:
                if s == d:
                    if cnt[d] >= 3:
                        ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos3[d]]
                        ans.sort()
                        print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                        return
                else:
                    ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos1[s]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return

    # If few small values, enumerate pairs of them.
    if small_distinct <= SMALL:
        small_vals = [v for v in range(1, small_limit + 1) if cnt[v]]
        m = len(small_vals)
        for i in range(m):
            b = small_vals[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, m):
                c = small_vals[j]
                a = X - b - c
                if 1 <= a <= X and cnt[a] > 0 and a != b and a != c:
                    ans = [pos1[a], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return
        print(-1)
        return

    # NTT convolution of small values
    L = 1
    while L <= X:
        L <<= 1
    a = [0] * L
    for v in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[v]:
            a[v] = 1

    ntt(a, False)
    for i in range(L):
        a[i] = a[i] * a[i] % MOD
    ntt(a, True)

    max_pair = 2 * small_limit

    for av in range(1, X + 1):
        if cnt[av] == 0:
            continue
        S = X - av
        if S < 2 or S > max_pair:
            continue
        coef = a[S]
        if coef == 0 or coef == 1:
            continue
        if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
            other = S - av
            if other != av and 1 <= other <= small_limit and cnt[other] > 0:
                continue

        lo = S - small_limit
        if lo < 1:
            lo = 1
        hi = S // 2
        if lo > hi:
            continue

        for b in range(lo, hi + 1):
            if cnt[b] == 0:
                continue
            c = S - b
            if c < 1 or c > small_limit or cnt[c] == 0:
                continue
            if b == c or b == av or c == av:
                continue
            ans = [pos1[av], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
            ans.sort()
            print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
            return

    print(-1)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's double-check syntax: In `next_int`, `data[p]` where data is bytes; okay. `data = None` after nested function; because `data` is a cell variable, assigning None is okay. But note: In Python, if a variable is assigned anywhere in a function, it's local. `data` is assigned before nested function and after; `next_int` closes over it. Good.

One potential issue: `data = None` after reading but `next_int` closure still references `data` cell. However, because `data` is assigned after the nested function, Python treats `data` as a local variable in main, and `next_int` uses it as a free variable. Assigning `data = None` is allowed. Good.

Another potential issue: `array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)` if X+1 is large, okay. But if X=0? no.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from array import array

MOD = 998244353
G = 3
SMALL = 5000


def ntt(a, invert):
    mod = MOD
    n = len(a)

    j = 0
    for i in range(1, n):
        bit = n >> 1
        while j & bit:
            j ^= bit
            bit >>= 1
        j ^= bit
        if i < j:
            a[i], a[j] = a[j], a[i]

    length = 2
    while length <= n:
        wlen = pow(G, (mod - 1) // length, mod)
        if invert:
            wlen = pow(wlen, mod - 2, mod)

        half = length >> 1
        for i in range(0, n, length):
            w = 1
            end = i + half
            for j in range(i, end):
                u = a[j]
                v = a[j + half] * w % mod

                x = u + v
                if x >= mod:
                    x -= mod

                y = u - v
                if y < 0:
                    y += mod

                a[j] = x
                a[j + half] = y
                w = w * wlen % mod

        length <<= 1

    if invert:
        inv_n = pow(n, mod - 2, mod)
        for i in range(n):
            a[i] = a[i] * inv_n % mod


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    if not data:
        return

    p = 0
    ndata = len(data)

    def next_int():
        nonlocal p
        while p < ndata and data[p] <= 32:
            p += 1
        num = 0
        while p < ndata and data[p] > 32:
            num = num * 10 + (data[p] - 48)
            p += 1
        return num

    N = next_int()
    X = next_int()

    small_limit = X // 2

    cnt = [0] * (X + 1)
    pos1 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)
    pos2 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)
    pos3 = array('i', [0]) * (X + 1)

    small_distinct = 0

    for pos in range(1, N + 1):
        v = next_int()
        c = cnt[v]

        if c == 0:
            if v <= small_limit:
                small_distinct += 1
            pos1[v] = pos
        elif c == 1:
            pos2[v] = pos
        elif c == 2:
            pos3[v] = pos

        cnt[v] = c + 1

    data = None

    # Triples with at least two equal values.
    for d in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[d] >= 2:
            s = X - 2 * d
            if 1 <= s <= X and cnt[s] >= 1:
                if s == d:
                    if cnt[d] >= 3:
                        ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos3[d]]
                        ans.sort()
                        print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                        return
                else:
                    ans = [pos1[d], pos2[d], pos1[s]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return

    # If the number of small values is small, enumerate their pairs.
    if small_distinct <= SMALL:
        small_vals = [v for v in range(1, small_limit + 1) if cnt[v]]
        m = len(small_vals)

        for i in range(m):
            b = small_vals[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, m):
                c = small_vals[j]
                a = X - b - c
                if 1 <= a <= X and cnt[a] > 0 and a != b and a != c:
                    ans = [pos1[a], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
                    ans.sort()
                    print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
                    return

        print(-1)
        return

    # Any valid triple has at least two values <= X // 2.
    # Compute pair sums of such small values by NTT.
    L = 1
    while L <= X:
        L <<= 1

    a = [0] * L
    for v in range(1, small_limit + 1):
        if cnt[v]:
            a[v] = 1

    ntt(a, False)
    for i in range(L):
        a[i] = a[i] * a[i] % MOD
    ntt(a, True)

    max_pair = 2 * small_limit

    for av in range(1, X + 1):
        if cnt[av] == 0:
            continue

        S = X - av
        if S < 2 or S > max_pair:
            continue

        coef = a[S]

        if coef == 0 or coef == 1:
            continue

        # coef == 2 or 3 may be caused only by a pair involving av.
        if coef == 2 or coef == 3:
            other = S - av
            if other != av and 1 <= other <= small_limit and cnt[other] > 0:
                continue

        lo = S - small_limit
        if lo < 1:
            lo = 1
        hi = S // 2

        if lo > hi:
            continue

        for b in range(lo, hi + 1):
            if cnt[b] == 0:
                continue

            c = S - b
            if c < 1 or c > small_limit or cnt[c] == 0:
                continue

            if b == c or b == av or c == av:
                continue

            ans = [pos1[av], pos1[b], pos1[c]]
            ans.sort()
            print(ans[0], ans[1], ans[2])
            return

    print(-1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
